each microservice owns its own MongoDB database (tenant‑isolated).  \n- **Overall Alignment Status**: ✅ ALL EXISTING PHASES REVIEWED & AUGMENTED – functional and non‑functional gaps closed, enterprise‑grade security, observability, and disaster‑recovery incorporated.\n\n## 🛠️ 2. Comprehensive System Architecture Phases\n*(All original phases retained in exact sequence; each augmented with concrete schemas, API contracts, state‑machine lifecycles, and messaging topology.)*\n\n### 🔹 Phase 1: User Management, Authentication, and Authorization\n- **Original Objective**: Implement User Management, Authentication, and Authorization.  \n- **Gap Analysis**: Original phase lacked explicit tenant provisioning, role‑based access control (RBAC), MFA enforcement, audit‑log schema, and a defined user lifecycle state machine. No messaging contract for user events.  \n- **Augmented Architecture & Solutions**:  \n  - **Data Models/Schema** (MongoDB collections):  \n    - **Tenants** (`tenant_id`, `name`, `status`, `subscription_tier`, `created_at`)  \n    - **Users** (`user_id`, `tenant_id`, `email`, `password_hash`, `role`, `mfa_enabled`, `created_at`, `last_login`)  \n    - **Sessions** (`session_id`, `user_id`, `tenant_id`, `jwt_token`, `refresh_token`, `issued_at`, `expires_at`, `revoked`)  \n    - **Roles** (`role_id`, `tenant_id`, `name`, `permissions`)  \n  - **API & Event Contracts** (Express routes, request/response payloads):  \n    - `POST /auth/signup` – `{email, password, companyName}` → `201 Created` with `user_id`  \n    - `POST /auth/login` – `{email, password, mfaCode}` → `200 OK` with `access_token`, `refresh_token`  \n    - `POST /auth/logout` – `Authorization: Bearer <token>` → `200 OK` (revokes session)  \n    - `GET /users/me` – `Authorization: Bearer <token>` → user profile  \n    - **Event Bus**: RabbitMQ exchange `user_events` (direct), queue `user_event_queue`, routing keys `user.created`, `user.verified`, `user.login`, `user.logout`. DLQ: `user_event_dlq`.  \n  - **State‑Machine Lifecycle** (User):  \n    - **UNVERIFIED** → `email_verification` trigger → **ACTIVE**  \n    - **ACTIVE** → `login` trigger → **ACTIVE** (session renewal)  \n    - **ACTIVE** → `deactivate` trigger → **SUSPENDED**  \n    - **SUSPENDED** → `reactivate` trigger → **ACTIVE**  \n    - **ACTIVE** → `delete_account` trigger → **DEACTIVATED** (soft‑delete)  \n  - **Security Controls**: Argon2id password hashing (salt\u202f16\u202fB, mem\u202f64\u202fMiB, iter\u202f3), JWT RS256 (24\u202fh expiry), MFA via TOTP/SMS, TLS\u202f1.3, AES‑256‑GCM for token encryption, OWASP Top‑10 mitigations, audit logs written to `AuditLogs` collection (see Phase\u202f4).  \n  - **Observability**: Prometheus counters (`auth_attempts_total`, `auth_success_total`), latency histograms (`auth_request_duration_seconds`), Grafana panels for sign‑up/login rates, ELK pipeline for auth‑related logs.\n\n### 🔹 Phase 2: Autonomous Pricing Engine, Inventory Sync, and Advertising Scheduler\n- **Original Objective**: Develop Autonomous Pricing Engine, Inventory Sync, and Advertising Scheduler.  \n- **Gap Analysis**: Original phase missing concrete micro‑service definitions, AI pricing engine contract, inventory webhook schema with optimistic locking, advertising scheduler UI/API, durable message‑queue for failures, and observability.  \n- **Augmented Architecture & Solutions**:  \n  - **Data Models/Schema** (per‑tenant MongoDB databases):  \n    - **Products** (`product_id`, `tenant_id`, `sku`, `name`, `price` (Decimal), `stock_quantity` (Int), `version` (Int), `status` (ACTIVE/OUT_OF_STOCK/DISCONTINUED), `last_updated`)  \n    - **PricingRules** (`rule_id`, `tenant_id`, `rule_name`, `conditions` (JSON), `actions` (JSON), `enabled`, `created_at`)  \n    - **PricingSuggestions** (`suggestion_id`, `tenant_id`, `product_id`, `suggested_price`, `confidence_score`, `generated_at`, `status` (PENDING/ACCEPTED/REJECTED))  \n    - **InventoryEvents** (`event_id`, `tenant_id`, `product_id`, `quantity_change`, `new_quantity`, `version`, `event_type` (IN/OUT/ADJUST), `timestamp`, `source`)  \n    - **Campaigns** (`campaign_id`, `tenant_id`, `name`, `ad_platform` (INSTAGRAM/FACEBOOK/GOOGLE_SHOPPING), `status` (DRAFT/SCHEDULED/RUNNING/COMPLETED/CANCELLED), `schedule_start`, `schedule_end`, `budget`, `created_by`, `created_at`)  \n    - **CreativeAssets** (`asset_id`, `tenant_id`, `campaign_id`, `file_name`, `file_path`, `file_size` (BigInt), `mime_type`, `uploaded_at`)  \n  - **API & Event Contracts**:  \n    - **Pricing Engine Service** (`./sources/backend/pricing-engine/`):  \n      - `POST /pricing/suggest` – `{product_id, competitor_prices[], demand_score}` → `202 Accepted` with `suggestion_id`  \n      - `GET /pricing/suggestions/{suggestion_id}` – polling endpoint  \n      - **Event**: `pricing.suggest` (routing key) published to RabbitMQ exchange `pricing_events`.  \n    - **Inventory Sync Service** (`./sources/backend/inventory-sync/`):  \n      - `POST /webhooks/inventory` – `{product_id, quantity, version, timestamp}` → `200 OK` or `409 Conflict` (optimistic lock)  \n      - `GET /products/{product_id}/stock` – cached response (Redis TTL\u202f30\u202fs)  \n      - **Event**: `inventory.delta` (routing key) to exchange `inventory_events`.  \n    - **Advertising Scheduler Service** (`./sources/backend/ad-scheduler/`):  \n      - `POST /campaigns` – `{name, ad_platform, schedule_start, schedule_end, budget, creative_ids[]}` → `201 Created` with `campaign_id`  \n      - `PUT /campaigns/{campaign_id}/status` – `{status}` (internal state transitions)  \n      - `GET /campaigns/{campaign_id}` – campaign details  \n      - **Event**: `scheduler.create` and `scheduler.execute` routed via exchange `ad_scheduler_events`.  \n  - **Messaging Topology** (RabbitMQ):  \n    - Exchanges: `pricing_events` (direct), `inventory_events` (direct), `ad_scheduler_events` (direct).  \n    - Queues: `pricing_queue`, `inventory_queue`, `scheduler_queue`.  \n    - Routing Keys: `pricing.suggest`, `inventory.delta`, `scheduler.create`, `scheduler.execute`.  \n    - DLQ Setup: each queue has a corresponding DLQ (`pricing_dlq`, `inventory_dlq`, `scheduler_dlq`) with `x-dead-letter-exchange` pointing to the primary exchange.  \n    - Consumer retries: exponential back‑off (base\u202f1\u202fs, max\u202f30\u202fs), max\u202f5 attempts before DLQ.  \n  - **State‑Machine Lifecycles**:  \n    - **PricingSuggestion**: `REQUESTED` → `PROCESSING` (AI model) → `COMPLETED`/`FAILED`.  \n    - **InventoryEvent**: `RECEIVED` → `VALIDATED` → `APPLIED` (update `Products`) → `CONFLICT` (if version mismatch).  \n    - **Campaign**: `DRAFT` → `SCHEDULED` → `RUNNING` (ad‑platform API call) → `COMPLETED`/`CANCELLED`.  \n  - **Security Controls**: JWT tenant isolation, row‑level security on all collections, AES‑256‑KMS at‑rest, TLS\u202f1.3, OWASP mitigations, input validation, rate limiting at API gateway.  \n  - **Observability**: Prometheus metrics (`pricing_suggestion_duration_seconds`, `inventory_webhook_latency_seconds`, `campaign_execution_success_total`), Grafana dashboards, ELK logs for pricing and inventory events, Jaeger traces for cross‑service flows.\n\n### 🔹 Phase 3: Analytics Dashboard, KPI Reporting, and Data Visualization\n- **Original Objective**: Build Analytics Dashboard, KPI Reporting, and Data Visualization.  \n- **Gap Analysis**: Original phase lacked data aggregation pipelines, real‑time dashboard updates, export functionality, and monitoring of analytical jobs.  \n- **Augmented Architecture & Solutions**:  \n  - **Data Models/Schema** (Analytics DB, per‑tenant):  \n    - **SalesMetrics** (`metric_id`, `tenant_id`, `date`, `total_sales`, `avg_discount`)  \n    - **PricingEffectiveness** (`eff_id`, `tenant_id`, `date`, `price_change_pct`, `revenue_impact`)  \n    - **InventoryTurnover** (`turn_id`, `tenant_id`, `date`, `units_sold`, `stock_at_end`)  \n    - **AdPerformance** (`perf_id`, `tenant_id`, `date`, `impressions`, `clicks`, `spend`, `conversions`)  \n    - **DashboardConfigs** (`config_id`, `tenant_id`, `widgets`, `refresh_interval`)  \n  - **API & Event Contracts** (`./sources/backend/analytics/`):  \n    - `GET /analytics/kpis?start={ISO}&end={ISO}` → JSON array of aggregated metrics  \n    - `GET /analytics/export?format=csv|pdf|excel` → file download (max\u202f50\u202fMB)  \n    - WebSocket `ws://analytics/stream` – real‑time push of latest metric batches  \n    - **Event**: `kpi.computed` published to exchange `kpi_events`.  \n  - **Messaging**: Queue `analytics_agg`, exchange `kpi_events`. DLQ `analytics_dlq`.  \n  - **State‑Machine** (Report Generation): `QUEUED` → `PROCESSING` → `GENERATED` → `FAILED`.  \n  - **Security**: JWT + RBAC (Analyst role), tenant isolation, audit logging.  \n  - **Observability**: Grafana panels for each KPI, alert thresholds (e.g., `pricing_effectiveness_delta > 20%`), Prometheus alerts, ELK for analytics job logs.\n\n### 🔹 Phase 4: Multi-Tenancy, Data Isolation, and Security Features\n- **Original Objective**: Implement Multi-Tenancy, Data Isolation, and Security Features.  \n- **Gap Analysis**: Original phase missing detailed tenant schema, tenant‑specific database provisioning, row‑level security enforcement, encryption key management, and comprehensive audit‑log schema.  \n- **Augmented Architecture & Solutions**:  \n  - **Data Models/Schema** (Tenant Management DB):  \n    - **Tenants** (`tenant_id`, `name`, `status` (ACTIVE/SUSPENDED/CLOSED), `subscription_tier`, `created_at`, `default_schema_version`)  \n    - **TenantSchemas** (`schema_id`, `tenant_id`, `collection_name`, `field_definitions` (JSON), `row_level_policies` (JSON))  \n    - **TenantUsers** (`tenant_user_id`, `tenant_id`, `user_id`, `role`, `assigned_at`)  \n    - **TenantSettings** (`tenant_id`, `encryption_key_id`, `audit_retention_days`, `allowed_network_cidrs`)  \n  - **API & Event Contracts** (`./sources/backend/tenant-mgmt/`):  \n    - `POST /tenants` – `{name, subscription_tier}` → `201 Created` with `tenant_id`  \n    - `GET /tenants/{tenant_id}` – tenant details (admin only)  \n    - `PUT /tenants/{tenant_id}/settings` – update security settings  \n    - **Event**: `tenant.provisioned`, `tenant.deactivated` routed via exchange `tenant_events`.  \n  - **Messaging**: Queue `tenant_mgmt_queue`, DLQ `tenant_mgmt_dlq`.  \n  - **State‑Machine** (Tenant Lifecycle): `PROVISIONING` → `ACTIVE` → `SUSPENDED` → `DECOMMISSIONED`.  \n  - **Security Controls**:  \n    - **Authentication**: JWT RS256, MFA (TOTP), password policy (12\u202fchars, complexity, no reuse).  \n    - **Authorization**: RBAC with roles (OWNER, ADMIN, MERCHANT, ANALYST).  \n    - **Encryption**: AES‑256‑KMS for at‑rest data; TLS\u202f1.3 for transport; key rotation every 90\u202fdays.  \n    - **OWASP**: Input validation, output encoding, SQL/NoSQL injection prevention, XSS mitigations, rate limiting at API gateway.  \n    - **Audit Logging**: Immutable `AuditLogs` collection (`audit_id`, `timestamp`, `tenant_id`, `user_id`, `session_id`, `action`, `resource`, `old_value`, `new_value`, `source_ip`, `user_agent`, `request_id`).  \n  - **Observability**: Tenant usage metrics (`tenant_active_users`), security event alerts (`failed_login_attempts > 10`), Grafana dashboards.\n\n### 🔹 Phase 5: Cross-System Integration, Performance Profiling, and Production Deployment\n- **Original Objective**: Cross-System Integration, Performance Profiling, and Production Deployment.  \n- **Gap Analysis**: Original phase lacked CI/CD pipeline definition, observability stack, performance profiling scripts, disaster‑recovery plan, and deployment topology.  \n- **Augmented Architecture & Solutions**:  \n  - **Data Models/Schema** (Infra & Ops DB):  \n    - **DeploymentConfigs** (`config_id`, `app_name`, `environment`, `docker_image`, `replicas`, `resources`, `health_checks`)  \n    - **MonitoringMetrics** (`metric_id`, `metric_name`, `value`, `timestamp`, `labels`)  \n    - **AlertRules** (`rule_id`, `alert_name`, `condition`, `severity`, `notification_channels`)  \n  - **API & Event Contracts** (`./sources/backend/deployment/`):  \n    - `POST /deployments` – trigger CI/CD pipeline (ArgoCD) → `202 Accepted` with pipeline ID  \n    - `GET /deployments/{pipeline_id}/status` – pipeline status  \n    - `GET /health` – service health check (returns `200 OK` if all dependencies healthy)  \n    - **Event**: `deployment.triggered`, `deployment.completed` routed via exchange `deployment_events`.  \n  - **Messaging**: Queue `integration_queue`, DLQ `integration_dlq`.  \n  - **State‑Machine** (Deployment Lifecycle): `BUILD` → `TEST` → `STAGING` → `PRODUCTION` → `ROLLBACK`.  \n  - **CI/CD Pipeline** (GitOps):  \n    - Source: Git repository (`./sources/backend/`, `./sources/frontend/`).  \n    - Build: Docker images (multi‑stage) → push to AWS ECR.  \n    - Test: Unit tests (Jest), integration tests (Supertest), security scans (Snyk).  \n    - Deploy: ArgoCD syncs manifests to EKS clusters across 2 AZs.  \n    - Rollback: Automated rollback on health‑check failure (`/health` >\u202f5\u202fs).  \n  - **Observability Stack**:  \n    - **Metrics**: Prometheus scrapes Node Exporter, application metrics (`express_requests_total`), pricing engine latency.  \n    - **Logs**: ELK (Filebeat → Logstash → Elasticsearch) with index rotation.  \n    - **Tracing**: Jaeger for distributed tracing across services.  \n    - **Alerting**: Prometheus Alertmanager → SNS → PagerDuty.  \n  - **High Availability & Disaster Recovery**:  \n    - **HA**: Kubernetes HPA based on CPU >\u202f70\u202f% or request latency >\u202f200\u202fms; L7 load balancer (ALB) with target groups across 2 AZs; Istio service mesh for traffic management.  \n    - **DR**: Active‑active across AWS regions (us‑east‑1, eu‑west‑1); Route\u202f53 health checks trigger DNS failover; daily automated backups (MongoDB PITR) retained 30\u202fdays; RTO ≤\u202f4\u202fh, RPO ≤\u202f5\u202fmin.  \n    - **Security**: Network policies, IAM roles for service accounts, secret management (AWS Secrets Manager), WAF rules, regular penetration testing.  \n\n### ➕ Phase 6: No‑code UI Builder (Appended Phase)\n- **Technical Justification**: The original blueprint omitted a dedicated No‑code UI Builder, which is required to support drag‑and‑drop campaign creation, rule editing, and tenant self‑service. Without this phase, the functional requirements for UI cannot be satisfied.  \n- **Concrete Technical Deliverables**:  \n  - **Frontend Micro‑frontends** (`./sources/frontend/ui-builder/`) built with React\u202f18 + TypeScript, Redux Toolkit for state, Material‑UI for components.  \n  - **Backend UI Service** (`./sources/backend/ui-service/`) exposing:  \n    - `GET /ui/rules` – list tenant pricing/inventory rules  \n    - `POST /ui/rules` – create rule definition (JSON schema)  \n    - `POST /ui/campaigns` – instantiate campaign from visual canvas  \n    - `POST /ui/assets` – validate and store creative assets (<\u202f5\u202fMB, allowed MIME)  \n  - **State‑Machine** (UI Builder Session): `DESIGN` → `VALIDATING` → `PUBLISHED` → `ERROR`.  \n  - **Messaging**: Exchange `ui_events` (direct), queue `ui_builder_queue`, routing keys `ui.rule.created`, `ui.campaign.created`, `ui.asset.uploaded`. DLQ `ui_builder_dlq`.  \n  - **Security**: JWT tenant isolation, file‑upload virus scan (AWS GuardDuty), audit logging to `AuditLogs`.  \n  - **Observability**: UI interaction tracking (Amplitude), error reporting (Sentry), Grafana panels for rule usage, campaign creation latency.  \n\n## 🛡️ 3. Enterprise Compliance, High Availability & Disaster Recovery\n- **Security Boundaries**:  \n  - API Gateway enforces mutual TLS, JWT validation, rate limiting (1000 req/min per tenant).  \n  - Service mesh (Istio) implements mTLS, traffic policies, and request tracing.  \n  - Each microservice runs with least‑privilege IAM roles; database access via MongoDB Atlas with tenant‑specific user credentials.  \n  - Encryption‑in‑transit: TLS\u202f1.3 with forward secrecy; encryption‑at‑rest: AES‑256‑KMS.  \n  - OWASP Top‑10 mitigations embedded in code (input validation, output encoding, SQL/NoSQL injection prevention, XSS, etc.).  \n- **Asynchronous Failover Logic**:  \n  - All critical async operations (pricing suggestions, inventory updates, ad‑platform calls) are published to RabbitMQ with persistent delivery.  \n  - Consumer processes messages with exponential back‑off (base\u202f1\u202fs, max\u202f30\u202fs). After 5 unsuccessful attempts, message is routed to DLQ.  \n  - DLQ consumers log failures to `AuditLogs`, trigger alerts via SNS/PagerDuty, and provide manual retry UI.  \n  - Circuit breaker pattern (Hystrix) for external dependencies; after 5 consecutive failures, circuit opens for 30\u202fs, returning fallback responses (stale data).  \n- **High Availability**:  \n  - Multi‑AZ Kubernetes clusters with replica sets; HPA based on CPU/memory/latency.  \n  - Load balancer (Application Load Balancer) distributes traffic; health checks every 30\u202fs.  \n  - Redis cluster for caching; data replication across AZs.  \n- **Disaster Recovery**:  \n  - Active‑active across two AWS regions; cross‑region replication for MongoDB (Atlas Global Clusters).  \n  - Automated daily snapshots and point‑in‑time recovery (PITR) retained 30\u202fdays.  \n  - RTO ≤\u202f4\u202fh, RPO ≤\u202f5\u202fmin.  \n  - Failover orchestrated via Route\u202f53 latency‑based routing; DNS TTL set to 60\u202fs for rapid propagation.  \n- **Compliance & Auditing**:  \n  - All audit events (user actions, price changes, inventory updates, campaign creations) are appended to immutable `AuditLogs` collection with SHA‑256 hash chaining.  \n  - Log retention policy: 365\u202fdays; encrypted storage; access limited to compliance role.  \n  - Quarterly penetration testing, vulnerability scanning, and SOC\u202f2 Type\u202fII audits.  \n\n--- \n\n*End of Enterprise Global Blueprint Report.*'}
